title: Why planning confined to finance limits business insight
date: 2026-01-14 (listing_visible_date; 'January 14, 2026')  url: https://inlumi.com/why-planning-confined-to-finance-limits-business-insight/  src: https://inlumi.com/blog/
source[listing_card]: January 14, 2026 • 5mins read Why planning confined to finance limits business insight How does ESG impact on integrated planning? When implementing ESG disclosure reporting with a target setting to meet the sustainability… Read more : Why planning confined to finance limits business insight
article: Why planning confined to finance limits business insight - inlumi Featured Why planning confined to finance limits business insight Featured Why planning confined to finance limits business insight 14 January 2026 [email protected] Share in: For many CFOs, planning is a mature and well-governed finance capability. Budgets, forecasts and variance analyses are embedded in reporting cycles, supported by defined processes, controls and systems. Yet despite this maturity, a recurring question remains: why do planning outcomes so often fail to translate into better business decisions? The answer is rarely found in the quality of financial models themselves. Instead, it lies in where planning is positioned. When planning remains confined to finance, it delivers financial consistency, but frequently lacks operational relevance. In an environment where speed, adaptability and forward-looking insight increasingly define the value of the CFO office, this disconnect is becoming more visible. The structural gap between operational and financial planning In many organisations, operational planning still sits outside finance. Sales teams forecast volumes. Operations plan capacity, resources and logistics. Finance then translates these inputs into revenue, margin and cost projections later in the cycle. While this approach feels logical, it creates parallel views of the future. Volume forecast

## 324. INLUMI AS (999291988) site https://inlumi.com/
title: Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office
date: 2026-01-19 (listing_visible_date; 'January 19, 2026')  url: https://inlumi.com/why-the-epm-landscape-is-quietly-defining-the-limits-of-the-cfo-office/  src: https://inlumi.com/blog/
source[listing_card]: January 19, 2026 • 5mins read Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office How does ESG impact on integrated planning? When implementing ESG disclosure reporting with a target setting to meet the sustainability… Read more : Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office
article: Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office - inlumi Featured Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office Featured Why the EPM landscape is quietly defining the limits of the CFO office 19 January 2026 [email protected] Share in: Across many finance organisations, the Enterprise Performance Management (EPM) landscape is the result of years of pragmatic decisions. Systems were implemented to solve specific challenges at a specific moment in time. Over time, these solutions became embedded in finance operations and, in many cases, largely unquestioned. As the CFO agenda for 2026 takes shape, that comfort is increasingly being tested. The question is no longer whether existing EPM tools still function, but whether they still enable the finance function to evolve in line with new regulatory, analytical and business demands. The hidden cost of doing nothing Maintaining the status quo in the EPM landscape often feels like the least disruptive option. Systems are stable, processes are familiar and the perceived risks of change are tangible. The cost of doing nothing, however, is less visible and steadily increasing. That cost typically materialises through: Growing reliance on manual workarounds and spreadsheets Increasing effort to reconcile finance, tax and sustainability views Slower response to new regulatory requirements Reduc

## 325. INLUMI AS (999291988) site https://inlumi.com/
title: Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making
date: 2026-01-28 (listing_visible_date; 'January 28, 2026')  url: https://inlumi.com/why-the-future-of-epm-is-about-convergence-and-real-time-decision-making/  src: https://inlumi.com/blog/
source[listing_card]: January 28, 2026 • 6mins read Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making How does ESG impact on integrated planning? When implementing ESG disclosure reporting with a target setting to meet the sustainability… Read more : Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making
article: Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making - inlumi Featured Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making Featured Why the future of EPM is about convergence and real-time decision-making 28 January 2026 [email protected] Share in: The CFO office is entering a decisive phase of structural change. Planning is moving beyond finance, operational decisions increasingly require immediate financial context, and expectations around transparency, explainability and speed continue to intensify. Insight is no longer produced periodically to support review; it is expected continuously to support decisions. For boards and executive teams, this changes the role of the CFO office fundamentally. The focus is shifting from explaining performance to shaping outcomes. CFOs are increasingly expected to connect operational reality, financial consequence and strategic intent in real time with confidence. source[listing_card]: February 5, 2026 • 4mins read Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) January 2026: On-Prem insights In this post, we explain what an Oracle CPU is (and is not). We also show where to find the… Read more : Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) January 2026: On-Prem insights
article: Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) January 2026: On-Prem insights - inlumi Featured Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) January 2026: On-Prem insights Featured , Oracle Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) January 2026: On-Prem insights 5 February 2026 [email protected] Share in: Oracle released its latest quarterly Critical Patch Update (CPU) on January 20, 2026 . If you run Oracle EPM 11.2 on-prem (Hyperion) , CPUs are part of your regular security maintenance. They help keep your platform secure and aligned with Oracle support expectations. In this post, we explain what an Oracle CPU is (and is not). We also show where to find the official bulletin. Finally, we share how to plan CPU patching in a controlled way. This helps you avoid confusion with functional EPM releases like 11.2.23 or the upcoming 11.2.24 . What is an Oracle CPU? An Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) is a bundle of security patches for supported on-premises Oracle products. Oracle uses CPUs to fix security vulnerabilities in Oracle code and in third-party components shipped with Oracle products. In practice, CPU advisories work cumulatively. Each quarter highlights what Oracle added since the last CPU. If you skipped one or more cycles, review the earlier advisories too. KEY TAKEAWAYS Oracle releases CPUs quarterly (third Tuesday of January, April, July, and October). CPU bulletins cover all Oracle components

source[listing_card]: March 24, 2026 • 4mins read Oracle HFM BSL: How to prepare for the move from VBScript Oracle has set a clear direction for HFM scripting. With BSL now available and the Native Script Engine scheduled to… Read more : Oracle HFM BSL: How to prepare for the move from VBScript
article: Oracle HFM BSL: How to prepare for the move from VBScript - inlumi Featured Oracle HFM BSL: How to prepare for the move from VBScript Featured , Oracle Oracle HFM BSL: How to prepare for the move from VBScript 24 March 2026 [email protected] Share in: Oracle has set a clear direction for HFM scripting . With BSL now available and the Native Script Engine scheduled to become the standard in October 2026, HFM customers should start assessing the impact on their rules and applications now. In this article, we break down the key Oracle dates, what the shift from VBScript to BSL means in practice, and how to approach the transition in a controlled way. What changed in Oracle HFM? In 2025, Oracle started reshaping the future of HFM scripting by introducing Native Script Engine support . The move is directly linked to Microsoft’s deprecation of VBScript, and as a result, it gives HFM customers a new path forward. At the centre of this change is Business Script Language (BSL) . Oracle positions BSL as familiar to existing HFM teams, using VBScript-style syntax within a defined scope and supporting most built-in VBScript functions. This matters not only because of the technology itself, but also because of the direction of travel: from October 2026, BSL is expected to become the standard script engine , and then by October 2027, Oracle is expected to phase out the Microsoft VBScript eng

source[feed_entry]: [feed categories: Featured, Oracle, AI, EPBCS, EPM, EPM Cloud, FCC, FCCS, GenAI, Oracle Cloud, PBCS, Planning] A Quick Refresher Oracle EPM Cloud is an integrated enterprise performance management platform built on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure. FCC handles financial consolidation and close — ownership structures, intercompany eliminations, multi-GAAP reporting, equity method accounting and currency translation. To utilize Artificial Intelligence with
article: AI in Oracle EPM Cloud - inlumi Featured AI in Oracle EPM Cloud Featured , Oracle AI in Oracle EPM Cloud 26 June 2026 Guido Share in: A Quick Refresher Oracle EPM Cloud is an integrated enterprise performance management platform built on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure. Planning handles budgeting and forecasting. FCC handles financial consolidation and close — ownership structures, intercompany eliminations, multi-GAAP reporting, equity method accounting and currency translation. They’re designed to work together inside the EPM platform, alongside modules like ARCS ( account reconciliation), Narrative Reporting, ESG Reporting, Profitability and Cost Management and Tax Reporting. To utilize Artificial Intelligence within Oracle EPM Cloud, you require an Enterprise subscription. Where AI Shows Up Today The clearest, most production-ready AI capability in the Oracle EPM stack lives in Planning. Predictive Planning, often referred to as Auto Predict, applies machine learning forecasting models directly to your planning data. Instead of manually picking a forecasting method and tuning it, Auto Predict tests multiple statistical algorithms against your historical data and automatically selects the best fit — accounting for seasonality, trend and outliers along the way. In practice, this means: • Faster forecast cycles, since planners aren’t manually building trend lines in spreadsheets •

article: Oracle Critical Patch Update (CPU) October 2026: Insights for EPM - inlumi Featured Featured , Oracle 26 October 2026 [email protected] Share in: Published 26 October 2026 Updated 28 October 2026 Oracle published the Oracle Critical Patch Update Advisory for October 2026 on 20 October 2026 . For Oracle EPM 11.2 on-prem , also known as Hyperion, the patching work lands where it usually does: in the infrastructure technology beneath the applications. Unlike the previous two CPUs, this advisory carries no Oracle Hyperion section at all, so you have to build the scope component by component. What makes this quarter worth a closer read is not the volume. Most people who open this CPU will misread three things. Essbase shows a long list of fixes. Java seems to be missing. And an issue that has frustrated Financial Reporting users since the summer has quietly changed status. None of those three means what it looks like at first glance. Key takeaways Essbase: a long list of fixes, but no Essbase patch for EPM 11.2 . Those versions are standalone releases that no EPM version ships. Java: no October entry in the EPM patch guidance, followed by two ad-hoc Security Alerts in the days after the CPU. Oracle has since added the October Java entry — see the update below. Financial Reporting Web Studio: the October level clears the issue that July 2026 introduced. The CPU applies to EPM 11.2.23
